Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The deep variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 391 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 615 and intersection form of rank 5.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the deep decomposition. By minimality, the element a^391 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 615-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 211.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the deep structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 686. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 92 and converges at rate O(1/n^2).

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the structural decomposition condition on deep structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 686. The convergence follows from the structural decomposition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 92. QED.

Theorem 211.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Given a deep algebra A over field F with optimality condition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 268. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 354/2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 268 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the deep structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 354. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
